# Daily Papers
The project automatically fetches the latest papers from arXiv based on keywords.

The subheadings in the README file represent the search keywords.

Only the most recent articles for each keyword are retained, up to a maximum of 100 papers.

You can click the 'Watch' button to receive daily email notifications.

Last update: 2026-11-10

## Multimodal Learning
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Towards Multi-Source Domain Generalization for Sleep Staging with Noisy Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10009v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Automatic sleep staging is a multimodal learning problem involving heterogeneous physiological signals such as EEG and EOG, which often suffer from domain shifts across institutions, devices, and populations. In practice, these data are also affected by noisy annotations, yet label-noise-robust multi-source domain generalization remains underexplored. We present the first benchmark for Noisy Labels in Multi-Source Domain-Generalized Sleep Staging (NL-DGSS) and show that existing noisy-label learning methods degrade substantially when domain shifts and label noise coexist. To address this challenge, we propose FF-TRUST, a domain-invariant multimodal sleep staging framework with Joint Time-Frequency Early Learning Regularization (JTF-ELR). By jointly exploiting temporal and spectral consistency together with confidence-diversity regularization, FF-TRUST improves robustness under noisy supervision. Experiments on five public datasets demonstrate consistent state-of-the-art performance under diverse symmetric and asymmetric noise settings.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to the MLESP 2026 workshop (The 7th international workshop on Machine Learning for EEG Signal Processing) in conjunction with IEEE BIBM 2026</p></details> |
| **[Exploring the Capabilities of Large Language Model Encoders for Image-Text Retrieval in Chest X-rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15234v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal learning from paired medical images and clinical text is a central challenge in medical data-driven informatics, where effective cross-modal alignment is critical for scalable analysis and retrieval. In chest radiography, vision-language pretraining is constrained by heterogeneous radiology reports that contain abbreviations, impression-only notes, and institution-specific writing styles. Unlike general-domain settings, naively aggregating large collections of noisy reports can plateau or even degrade multimodal learning when reporting styles differ substantially. We propose a domain-adapted bidirectional large language model text encoder for chest radiograph reports, trained with masked token prediction and supervised contrastive learning on stylistically diverse but clinically equivalent report variants to produce robust, generalizable text embeddings. We then integrate this encoder into a dual-tower contrastive vision-language framework using parameter-efficient adaptation to improve image-text alignment. Across 1.6 million paired studies from public datasets and a de-identified hospital cohort, the proposed models improve bidirectional retrieval accuracy and external generalization, achieving GREEN scores of 0.308 on MIMIC-CXR and 0.618 on Open-I, while reducing the degradation observed when abbreviation-rich, impression-only hospital reports are added to training.</p></details> | <details><summary>12 pa...</summary><p>12 pages, 2 figures, under review</p></details> |
| **[Conditional Diffusion-Based Proxy Representation Recovery for BCVA Prediction with Missing SLO Modality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11326v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Preoperative prediction of postoperative best-corrected visual acuity (BCVA) can support prognosis assessment and individualized decision-making in cataract surgery. Multimodal learning can integrate preoperative optical coherence tomography (OCT) images, scanning laser ophthalmoscopy (SLO) images, and clinical text to characterize patient status. However, SLO images are often missing in real-world clinical data due to acquisition or data-management issues, resulting in incomplete multimodal inputs. To address this problem, this paper proposes a conditional diffusion-based method for SLO proxy representation recovery and fusion prediction. Instead of reconstructing raw SLO images, the proposed method formulates SLO missingness as proxy representation recovery in the latent feature space. Specifically, OCT image features and clinical text features are first fused to construct an OCT-text conditional representation, which guides the reverse denoising process of the diffusion model to estimate sample-specific SLO proxy representations. The recovered SLO proxy representation is then used as a substitute for the unavailable SLO modality and incorporated into the downstream multimodal fusion prediction network for postoperative BCVA prediction. Experiments were conducted on 2,141 preoperative multimodal cataract samples, with an SLO missing rate of 58.80%. The proposed method achieved an accuracy of 0.9714 under the criterion of an absolute prediction error within 0.1 LogMAR and a mean absolute error of 0.0423, outperforming representative postoperative visual acuity prediction and missing-modality learning methods. These results indicate that representation-level proxy recovery can alleviate the impact of SLO modality missingness on postoperative BCVA prediction.</p></details> | <details><summary>8 pag...</summary><p>8 pages, 3 figures, 3 tables. Accepted at IEEE BIBM 2026</p></details> |
| **[Neural Signals Generate Clinical Notes in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22197v4)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Generating clinical reports that summarize abnormal patterns, diagnostic findings, and clinical interpretations from long-term EEG recordings remains labor-intensive. We present CELM, the first clinical EEG-to-Language foundation model capable of summarizing long-duration, variable-length EEG recordings and performing end-to-end clinical report generation at multiple scales. CELM integrates pretrained EEG foundation models with language models to enable scalable multimodal learning. We curate a large-scale clinical EEG dataset containing 9,922 reports paired with approximately 11,000 hours of EEG recordings from 9,048 patients to train CELM, and release the benchmark pipeline, with automated report-structuring, to facilitate future research. Experimental results show that CELM consistently outperforms existing methods across all evaluation settings. Importantly, we further conduct human evaluation with clinical experts, demonstrating that CELM generates reports that are more clinically coherent, diagnostically reliable, and better aligned with expert interpretation. We release our model code and benchmark construction pipeline at https://github.com/Jathurshan0330/CELM.</p></details> |  |
| **[LAION-BVD: A 13-Million-Hour Open Video Dataset for Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24845v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present LAION-BVD (LAION - Big Video Dataset), a large-scale open video dataset for multimodal learning, which contains 1.3B platform-specific video URLs collected from CommonCrawl. From these, we download 80M videos with a total duration of 13.5 million hours. The dataset is designed for multimodal pre-training across the video, audio, and image modalities. Using content-aware scene detection, we extract clips for which we synthetically generate video and audio captions. Models trained on these data achieve competitive performance on standard video-text and audio-text benchmarks, with consistent improvements as training or model scale increases. Additionally, we explore video frames as an alternative source of image-text data by extracting scene-changing frames. These frames exhibit a visual distribution distinct from standard web image corpora, and models trained on this dataset achieve strong image-text retrieval performance. We release LAION-BVD to the research community. It significantly expands open access to multimodal videos at an unprecedented scale.</p></details> |  |
| **[MM-DyGraph: A Dataset, Benchmark, and Model for Multimodal Dynamic Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09240v1)** | 2026-11-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal dynamic graphs are pervasive in real-world applications, where nodes and edges are accompanied by multimodal signals, including text and images. Despite their practical importance, benchmarks and methods that explicitly study multimodal learning in dynamic graphs remain scarce, hindering the full understanding and effective utilization of multimodal information for downstream tasks. To address this gap, we introduce MM-DyGraph, to the best of our knowledge, the first benchmark for multimodal dynamic graph learning, consisting of diverse real-world datasets with fine-grained temporal graph evolution and rich multimodal attributes. MM-DyGraph provides three evaluation tasks to comprehensively assess model performance in dynamic settings. We benchmark representative state-of-the-art dynamic graph methods and reveal that naive feature concatenation yields target-level inconsistencies: multimodal inputs benefit certain prediction targets while harming others. This observation indicates that the modality informativeness depends on the specific prediction target over time. Building on these observations, we propose a Target-aware Spatial-Temporal Multimodal Fusion (TST-MF) framework, which jointly conditions multimodal fusion on the prediction target and spatial-temporal relations, enabling context-adaptive cross-modal interplay for target-specific representations. Extensive experiments over diverse datasets and tasks show that TST-MF delivers robust and competitive performance, achieving clear gains in multimodal settings.</p></details> |  |
| **[Robust Multimodal Learning via Entropy-Gated Contrastive Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15417v2)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real-world multimodal systems routinely face missing-input scenarios, and in reality, robots lose audio in a factory or a clinical record omits lab tests at inference time. Standard fusion layers either preserve robustness or calibration but never both. We introduce Adaptive Entropy-Gated Contrastive Fusion (AECF), a single light-weight layer that (i) adapts its entropy coefficient per instance, (ii) enforces monotone calibration across all modality subsets, and (iii) drives a curriculum mask directly from training-time entropy. On AV-MNIST and MS-COCO, AECF improves masked-input mAP by +18 pp at a 50% drop rate while reducing ECE by up to 200%, yet adds 1% run-time. All back-bones remain frozen, making AECF an easy drop-in layer for robust, calibrated multimodal inference.</p></details> | <details><summary>arXiv...</summary><p>arXiv admin note: This submission has been withdrawn because it does not meet arXiv's research content quality standards</p></details> |
| **[InfMasking: A Unified Variational Framework for Synergistic Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07702v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal models are expected to exploit synergistic information, that is, information available only when multiple modalities are observed together. In practice, however, a fusion module can fit the training objective by relying on a dominant modality or on information that modalities share, and the synergy between modalities is often left unused. We propose InfMasking, a unified multimodal learning framework based on Infinite Masking. Before fusion, InfMasking randomly masks the features of each modality; each sampled mask produces a different partial combination of cross-modal evidence and a fused representation of the corresponding modality interactions. All masked fused representations are required to remain consistent with a reference target: the unmasked fused view when training is unsupervised, and the class proxy of the task label when labels are available. Maintaining this consistency across diverse partial combinations discourages reliance on a dominant modality or on shared information alone, and drives the fusion module to learn cross-modal interaction patterns that remain meaningful under partial observations. Since the objective is an expectation over infinitely many masks, we model the masked fused representations as Gaussian and optimize closed-form variational bounds, whose population-moment gaps are controlled by the masking-induced covariance under this model. On the evaluated benchmarks, InfMasking improves predictive performance and yields higher mean PID estimates of task-label synergy in learned modality-specific representations, alongside higher InterSHAP interaction attribution in the trained predictors. Code is available at https://github.com/brightest66/InfMasking.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence (TPAMI). 16 pages; supplementary material provided as an ancillary file. Code: https://github.com/brightest66/InfMasking</p></details> |
| **[VidUEU-Agent: A Data-Curation Agent for Multimodal Understanding, Editing, and Unified Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04418v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The advancement of multimodal learning is heavily constrained by the scarcity and high annotation cost of high-quality instruction data. While raw videos offer an abundant and dynamic source of visual knowledge, transforming them into precise instruction data remains highly challenging. To bridge this gap, we present VidUEU-Agent, an automated pipeline that synthesizes instruction data from raw videos for multimodal understanding, editing, and unified tasks. VidUEU-Agent follows a four-stage pipeline: it performs global perception to filter low-quality shots, mines keyframes and multimodal signals, constructs metadata and routes the metadata to synthesize instruction samples. More importantly, our agent framework can autonomously iterate the data distribution based on downstream training results, thereby optimizing data quality, balance, and training effectiveness. Scaling this pipeline, we construct VidUEU-100K, a large-scale dataset of 100K training samples spanning multiple domains and tasks, providing a robust foundation for developing comprehensive multimodal capabilities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that fine-tuning baseline models on VidUEU-100K yields significant and consistent performance gains across diverse representative benchmarks, validating the effectiveness of our proposed agent framework and dataset.</p></details> |  |
| **[Probe-Guided Gradient Balancing for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02651v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Jointly trained multimodal networks frequently underutilize weaker modalities, as faster-learning modalities dominate the shared fusion objective and suppress gradients reaching slower ones. Most existing approaches throttle dominant modalities, whereas directly boosting weaker modalities often fails because monitoring signals derived from the joint loss remain dominated by stronger modalities. We propose Probe-Guided Gradient Boosting (PGGB), a gradient modulation approach that boosts weaker modalities while preserving the strength of dominant ones. PGGB attaches lightweight linear classifiers to stop-gradient features from each modality to estimate representation quality independently. The gap between these scores, termed the utilization gap, provides an imbalance signal used to adaptively scale weaker-modality gradients through bounded and smoothed modulation. PGGB is composable with throttling methods, as it leaves the loss function and fusion architecture unchanged. Experiments across eight benchmarks spanning four domains and 2-4 modalities demonstrate that PGGB outperforms state-of-the-art methods on highly imbalanced multimodal datasets while remaining competitive under low or negligible imbalance. We further establish bounded scaling, self-attenuation, and a descent bound for standard SGD under smoothness and bounded-variance assumptions. Code is available at https://github.com/VuVoCodes/PGGB.</p></details> |  |
| **[Fusion or Confusion? Multimodal Complexity Is Not All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22991v4)** | 2026-10-30 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal learning has become a prominent research area, with the potential of substantial performance gains by combining information across modalities. At the same time, model development has trended toward increasingly complex deep learning architectures, motivated by the assumption that multimodal-specific methods improve performance. We challenge this assumption through a large-scale empirical study by reimplementing 19 high-impact multimodal methods across nine diverse datasets with up to 23 modalities. Under standardized experimental conditions, including hyperparameter tuning, weight initialization, cross-validation, and statistical testing, increased multimodal complexity often yields confusion rather than effective fusion of data modalities. Accordingly, complex multimodal architectures do not reliably outperform unimodal baselines and a Simple Baseline for Multimodal Learning (SimBaMM). Through a focused case study, we further demonstrate concrete methodological shortcomings even in top-tier multimodal learning publications, underscoring the need for standardized evaluation practices. In summary, we argue for a shift in focus for multimodal learning: away from the pursuit of architectural novelty and toward methodological rigor.</p></details> |  |
| **[Soil Moisture Estimation in Precision Agriculture: A Knowledge Distillation Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40432v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate soil moisture estimation is critical for precision agriculture, influencing irrigation, fertilization, and harvesting strategies. Traditional methods primarily rely on meteorological and soil property data, while recent approaches incorporate visual modalities such as aerial and geospatial imagery. However, these high-resolution images are expensive and computationally demanding to analyze. In this work, we propose Soil Moisture Distillation (SMD), a Knowledge Distillation (KD)-based multimodal learning framework that leverages unimodal teachers to enhance multimodal soil moisture estimation. SMD surpasses not only traditional multimodal fusion techniques, including MIS-ME, but also outperforms unimodal models, demonstrating the benefits of guided unimodal knowledge transfer. Additionally, we conduct a comparative analysis of soil patches and crop images, showing that crop images provide superior visual cues, leading to improved prediction accuracy. SMD achieves a 2% improvement in MAPE for soil patches and 1.2% for crops over state-of-the-art baselines, highlighting the effectiveness of leveraging both meteorological and visual data through knowledge distillation for enhanced soil moisture forecasting.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted by MWAIS 2025</p></details> |
| **[IMPACT: Inverter-Informed Multimodal Learning for Photovoltaic Generation Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40418v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This work develops a physics-informed multimodal deep learning framework for 10- to 110-minute-ahead probabilistic photovoltaic power forecasting. The forecasting model is built on a four-branch architecture that separately encodes plant-level temporal history and inverter-level spatial cloud dynamics through a graph attention encoder with a learned adjacency, known-future solar geometry, and the current meteorological state, fused through gated cross-attention. The geometry branch adds two forward-looking physical signals, spatial cloud heterogeneity and inverter ramp-down activity, each empirically weighted toward the horizon where it is most predictive. The decoder anchors its prediction to a smart persistence baseline, learning a horizon-conditioned correction, and the resulting quantile forecasts are calibrated online through a kernel-weighted adaptive conformal calibration step. The proposed model outperforms all 14 baselines, achieving a forecast skill score (FSS) of 0.2950, 0.099 above the next-best model. A branch-composition ablation shows that every branch contributes positively and the model retains substantial skill in challenging overcast and variable sky conditions (FSS 0.198 and 0.255).</p></details> |  |
| **[Tackling Multimodal Learning Challenges with Mixture-of-Expert: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27431v2)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) presents a naturally compatible and scalable framework for multimodal learning, demonstrating strong adaptability across diverse modalities and tasks. Despite its growing success, a comprehensive and systematic review on the MoE metho addressing multimodal challenges remains lacking. Existing surveys tend to evaluate either multimodal learning or MoE independently from method taxonomy, overlooking the unique interplay between them. This survey fills that gap by answering a central question: \textit{How does MoE effectively resolve multimodal challenges?} We approach this from three key perspectives: (1) \textbf{MoE as an Efficient Multimodal Engine:} enabling scalable multimodal modeling by decoupling computational cost from parameter growth and mitigating modality redundancy through selective expert activation; (2) \textbf{MoE as a Multimodal Representation Learner:} integrating complementary multi-opinion expert knowledge to enrich alignment and interaction representations; and (3) \textbf{MoE as a Multimodal Adapter:} providing a modular and flexible mechanism to model imperfect data scenarios such as modality imbalance and missing modality. Through our extensive literature review, we identify critical research gaps, including interpretable routing, expert communication, modality integration, and lifelong multimodal learning. We position this survey as a foundation for future research toward interpretable and sustainable multimodal Mixture-of-Experts system.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This survey has been accepted by IJCAI 2026. Results were released by 30 April 2026. This latest replacement is an extension version of survey to the previous version, which has been submitted to ACM Computing Survey for peer-review</p></details> |
| **[Grounding Long Financial Text with Semantically-Enriched Tables via Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37913v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a multimodal transformer framework for efficiently modeling long1 financial documents that combine structured tabular data with noisy, unstructured2 textual disclosures. The framework represents financial tables as semantically3 enriched token sequences and integrates them with long-form text through a cross-4 attention mechanism that explicitly captures cross-modal dependencies. In par-5 ticular, we augment each numerical variable with a semantic embedding derived6 from its textual definition, enabling the model to encode both numerical magnitude7 and economic meaning. This design yields a more expressive and parameter-8 efficient representation of tabular data compared to value-only embeddings. Using9 a large-scale dataset of financial statements and MD&A disclosures, we show that10 structured numerical features remain the dominant source of predictive signal;11 however, textual information provides consistent incremental gains when incor-12 porated via cross-attention rather than simple concatenation. Our results further13 demonstrate that noisy textual disclosures are most useful when conditioned on a14 stable, semantically structured tabular representation. In particular, using frozen15 variable-level tabular representations as cross-attention queries enables the model16 to weight MD&A chunks differently for each financial variable and observation.17 These findings suggest that cross-attention provides an efficient mechanism for18 table-conditioned aggregation of long-form narrative evidence.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to Neurips Long-context foundation model workshop</p></details> |
| **[Task-Centric Multimodal Learning for Cognitive Impairment Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20906v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dementia affects millions of older adults worldwide, yet early detection remains difficult outside clinical settings. Traditional models primarily rely on text or voice-based responses, capturing only a limited view of human cognition despite the inherently multimodal nature of human expression. This work proposes a contactless, multimodal screening framework that leverages video, audio, and text signals collected during structured voice-based interactions with Amazon Alexa. By incorporating these modalities, our approach captures a more holistic representation of human behavior encompassing speech patterns, vocal characteristics, facial expressions, and linguistic structure, which may enable more accurate detection of cognitive decline. Different participants completed three conversational tasks designed to probe spontaneous speech, comprehension, and recall. We pursued three objectives: (1) evaluate which of the three Alexa tasks provides the most informative cognitive signals, (2) compare the effectiveness of unimodal models, and (3) assess the benefits of multimodal fusion models. Task-level analysis shows that the video model achieves its best performance on Command-Reading, with an accuracy of 0.65 (AUC 0.69), while the audio model performs best on Question-Answering, reaching an accuracy of 0.67 (AUC 0.73). In contrast, the multimodal model demonstrates consistently stable performance across tasks, achieving an accuracy of 0.76 (AUC 0.75) on Command-Reading and 0.76 (AUC 0.73) on Question-Answering. These findings demonstrate that multimodal behavioral cues captured through naturalistic interactions can reliably distinguish MCI from healthy aging, highlighting the limitations of traditional diagnostic paradigms and supporting the shift toward multimodal, real-world cognitive assessment frameworks.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted for publication at the 2026 IEEE Global Communications Conference (GLOBECOM)</p></details> |
| **[MultiFair: Multimodal Balanced Fairness-Aware Medical Classification with Dual-Level Gradient Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07328v3)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Medical decision systems increasingly rely on data from multiple sources to ensure reliable and unbiased diagnosis. However, existing multimodal learning models fail to achieve this goal because they often overlook two critical challenges. First, various data modalities may learn unevenly, thereby converging to a model biased towards certain modalities. Second, the model may emphasize learning on certain demographic groups causing unfair performances. The two aspects can influence each other, as different data modalities may favor respective groups during optimization, leading to both imbalanced and unfair multimodal learning. This paper proposes a novel approach called MultiFair for multimodal medical classification, which addresses these challenges with a dual-level gradient modulation process. MultiFair dynamically modulates training gradients regarding the optimization direction and magnitude at both data modality and group levels. We evaluate MultiFair on three real-world medical classification datasets with diverse demographic attributes,including multiclass classification and missing-modality settings. Experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This work has been accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Medical Imaging</p></details> |
| **[Peers4U: Designing Learner-Centered Virtual Peers for Children's Multimodal Mathematics Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37238v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In early mathematics, children learn abstract concepts through concrete representations, such as manipulating objects, drawing, and speaking. Learning with peers can enrich this process by exposing children to diverse strategies and enabling them to exchange ideas through interaction. We realized this potential with virtual peers in Peers4U, a multimodal learning system to enhance children's mathematical conceptual understanding. Virtual peers driven by LLMs are personalized to the learner's cognitive level and assigned multimodal solutions with inspectable processes. During learning, peers engage in mixed-initiative interactions by responding, initiating topics, and advancing discussions along a pedagogical progression. A user study with 22 children and two experts showed our learner-centered topic orchestration significantly outperformed a persona-centered baseline, and children spontaneously authored new artifacts to demonstrate inspired ideas or share their own strategies, producing significantly more solutions when grouped with more Leading Peers. We contribute design insights for multimodal, learner-centered virtual peer systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[ML-ECS: Heterogeneity-Aware Collaborative Multimodal Learning for Edge-Cloud Synergies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14107v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Edge-cloud synergies provide a promising paradigm for privacy-preserving deployment of foundation models in heterogeneous web environments, enabling lightweight edge models to support domain-specific multimodal we b services while cloud-hosted models facilitate collaborative knowledge sharing. However, in practical edge-cloud web environments, collaborative multimodal learning is challenged by modality heterogeneity (different modality combinations across domains) and model-structure heterogeneity (different modality-specific encoders/fusion modules). To address these issues, we propose ML-ECS, a collaborative multimodal learning framework that enables joint training between a server-based model and heterogeneous edge models. This framework consists of four components: (1) cross-modal contrastive learning (CCL) to align modality representations in a shared latent space, (2) adaptive multimodal tuning (AMT) to preserve domain-specific knowledge from local datasets, (3) modality-aware aggregation (MAA) to robustly aggregate while mitigating noise caused by missing modalities, and (4) SLM-enhanced CCL (SE-CCL) to facilitate bidirectional knowledge transfer between cloud and edge. Experimental results on various multimodal tasks show that ML-ECS consistently outperform state-of-the-art baselines under varying modality availability, achieving improvements of 1.55% to 16.75% in Rouge-LSum and improving both client- and server-side performance. In addition, by communicating only low-rank LoRA parameters and fused representations, ML-ECS achieves high communication efficiency, requiring only 0.71% of the total parameter volume.</p></details> |  |
| **[VaViT: Vanilla ViT for Automotive Point Cloud Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31177v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Plain Transformers have become the de-facto architecture for processing text, audio, image, and video, offering a unified backbone for multimodal learning. However, state-of-the-art architectures for point cloud semantic segmentation remain dominated by U-Nets architectures where convolutions are interleaved with local or windowed attentions. In this work, we show how to effectively leverage vanilla, non-hierarchical ViTs for the segmentation of large-scale automotive lidar scenes. We bridge the performance gap thanks to a carefully designed tokenizer, a lightweight decoder segmentation head, and tailored data augmentations. Our approach, VaViT, for Vanilla ViT, matches or exceeds the performance of state-of-the-art methods while maintaining the simplicity of ViT architecture. We provide extensive evaluations on nuScenes, SemanticKITTI, and Waymo Open Dataset to validate the efficiency of our method. Code and pre-trained models are available at https://github.com/valeoai/VaViT.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |
| **[Ampe-DB: A Dataset for Human Activity and Interaction Analysis Using the Ghanaian Game Ampe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33130v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Human activity recognition benchmarks underrepresent culturally situated forms of movement and interaction. We introduce Ampe-DB, a multimodal dataset based on Ampe, a traditional Ghanaian game involving coordinated jumping, leg movements, and two-person interaction. It contains 637 canonical clips from 15 participants, with RGB video, two-person pose representations, participant and pair metadata, four-class movement labels, and derived binary movement-relation labels. We evaluate four-class movement recognition and binary movement-relation classification using three protocols: a duplicate-safe fixed benchmark, four-fold pair-held-out evaluation, and participant-held-out diagnostics for participants with complete class coverage. Baselines comprise a frozen Kinetics-400-pretrained R(2+1)D-18 RGB encoder, a two-layer bidirectional GRU for pose sequences, and validation-selected RGB-pose late fusion. For four-class recognition, RGB macro-F1 falls from 0.955 on the fixed benchmark to 0.418 under pair-held-out evaluation and 0.384 under participant-held-out evaluation. Pose is more robust, achieving 0.957, 0.811, and 0.839 across the same protocols. Fusion reaches 0.976 on the fixed benchmark and 0.824 under pair-held-out evaluation, but does not consistently improve on pose under participant shift. Binary movement-relation recognition also becomes substantially harder under held-out evaluation, especially for different-leg configurations. These findings show that strong fixed-benchmark scores can mask failures to generalize across people and pairings. Ampe-DB supports research on fine-grained movement recognition, human-human interaction, multimodal learning, and identity-aware generalization.</p></details> | <details><summary>74 pa...</summary><p>74 pages, 9 figures, 7 numbered tables. Ampe-DB V1 dataset (637 interaction clips) available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/RuthBiney/Ampe-DB</p></details> |
| **[Reliability-Aware Probabilistic Fusion for Multimodal Learning with Incomplete Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29621v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal inputs may be missing or degraded, raising distinct questions of evidence availability and trust. We introduce a reliability-aware Gaussian Product-of-Experts (PoE) in which an input-dependent reliability weight tempers each modality's latent precision, while missing experts are omitted. We characterize how reliability and availability affect posterior information and expert influence. Across two clinical multimodal tasks, the full framework remains competitive with complete inputs and achieves its clearest mean-AUROC advantage under modality absence, particularly on HAIM-MIMIC-MM. Ablations show a substantial gain from missing-aware training and an additional HAIM improvement from reliability weighting under matched PoE, missing-aware, and consistency training. Unseen test-time corruptions are used diagnostically and reveal modality-dependent reliability-weight and latent-uncertainty responses, including failure under severe degradation. Thus, reliability weighting provides an explicit evidence-control mechanism, while learning dependable quality signals remains an open challenge.</p></details> |  |
| **[On the Complementarity of Quantum and Classical Features: Adaptive Hybrid Quantum-Classical Feature Fusion for Breast Cancer Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22903v2)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The integration of quantum machine learning with classical deep learning offers promising avenues for medical image analysis by mapping data into high-dimensional Hilbert spaces. However, unifying these paradigms remains challenging due to optimization asymmetries. We propose a hybrid quantum-classical architecture for breast cancer diagnosis based on a dual-branch feature-extraction pipeline. Our framework unifies complementary representations from classical models and trainable or deterministic (non-trainable) quantum circuits. Three fusion strategies are introduced: Static Hybrid Fusion (SHF) for offline extraction, Dynamic Hybrid Fusion (DHF) for end-to-end co-adaptation, and Temperature-Scaled Hybrid Fusion (TSHF). Inspired by multimodal learning, TSHF uses a learnable scalar to balance branch gradient dynamics and address optimization bottlenecks. We evaluate BreastMNIST, INbreast, and BUS-UCLM, spanning ultrasound and mammography, using ResNet-18 and a shallow CNN, with five independent runs per configuration. Fusion improves over the strongest classical baseline on all three benchmarks. | **[Beyond Invariance: Factor-wise Selective Treatment of Shifts for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21403v2)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Distribution shifts pose a fundamental challenge to robust multimodal learning. The sequential nature of the Transformer architectures provides a natural framework for modeling such data, but introduces additional considerations, such as how temporal information should be represented. In this study, we systematically investigate these design choices using three different cancer datasets. We compare modality-level and feature-level tokenization and evaluate attention masking and imputation as strategies for handling missing information, both naturally occurring in the datasets and synthetically induced. We further investigate approaches for incorporating temporal information in our training process and how to model it. Experiments are conducted with unimodal and multimodal configurations, as well as different levels of missingness for binary survival classification and multiple time encoding strategies for discrete-time survival prediction. Our results show that while multimodal outperforms unimodal results, missing data heavily impacts predictions and thus effective design choices such as tokenization granularity, and intra-missing handling is essential for reliable prediction.</p></details> |  |
| **[Linear Multi-granular Body Modeling for Text-Driven Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06897v3)** | 2026-10-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Text-to-motion generation is a rapidly growing field at the nexus of multimodal learning and computer graphics, enabling flexible and cost-effective applications in gaming, animation, robotics, and virtual reality. | **[Constant-Curvature Sliced Gromov-Wasserstein for Heterogeneous Cross-Curvature Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07218v1)** | 2026-10-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent advances in representation learning have highlighted the utility of constant-curvature models, such as hyperbolic and spherical spaces, for modeling complex data. Consequently, they lack explicit mechanisms to enforce geometric consistency across various spaces. Moreover, the problem of comparing probability distributions across mixed-curvature spaces remains unexplored. To compare distributions on heterogeneous spaces, Gromov-Wasserstein (GW) distances provide a principled framework by aligning their intra-space geometries. Building on this, we propose constant-curvature sliced Gromov-Wasserstein (CCSGW), a novel divergence for aligning distributions supported on heterogeneous constant-curvature spaces. We first introduce the missing geodesic-based one-dimensional projections for spherical spaces, and then extend sliced GW to constant-curvature spaces, enabling efficient and principled comparison across manifolds with different curvatures. This formulation preserves intrinsic geometric relationships while avoiding the high computational cost. We provide theoretical analysis showing that CCSGW controls intrinsic geometric discrepancy across heterogeneous spaces, promoting distribution-level geometric consistency. By integrating CCSGW into existing mixed-curvature learning tasks, including graph anomaly detection, graph node classification, and multimodal learning, we observe consistent performance gains across diverse settings.</p></details> |  |

| **[From Models to Systems: A Comprehensive Survey of Efficient Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19445v1)** | 2026-09-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The rapid expansion of multimodal models has surfaced formidable bottlenecks in computation, memory, and deployment, catalyzing the rise of Efficient Multimodal Learning (EML) as a pivotal research frontier. Moving beyond a purely categorical review, we offer a methodological synthesis of the vertical synergies between these layers, elucidating how cross-layer co-design contributes to the fundamental "Efficiency-Utility-Privacy" trade-off. Through an integrative case study of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), we trace the field's evolutionary trajectory from initial structural adjustments to modern full-stack resource orchestration. Furthermore, we provide a holistic discussion and application-specific optimization blueprints for diverse domains and posit a paradigm shift toward self-regulating intelligence, where efficiency is an intrinsic, emergent property of the model's fundamental design rather than a post-hoc constraint. Finally, we present open challenges and future directions that will define the trajectory of EML research. We further discuss how emerging multimodal foundation models instantiate these principles at scale and identify open challenges including scalable information estimation in high-dimensional settings, standardized evaluation across information-theoretic methods, combinatorial complexity of multimodal PID, and the transition from post-hoc information analysis toward information-aware multimodal learning.</p></details> |  |

| **[Semantics at an Angle: When Cosine Similarity Works Until It Doesn't](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16318v4)** | 2026-08-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Cosine similarity is a standard comparison rule for learned representations in information retrieval, natural language processing, computer vision, and multimodal learning. ## Representation Learning
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Rethinking Radar 3D Perception: Representation Learning from Raw Spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12334v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Raw radar spectra are widely viewed as too cluttered and noisy for direct representation learning, motivating handcrafted reductions, sparse point clouds, or compact Doppler descriptors in radar perception. We challenge this assumption and argue that the main limitation lies not in the raw representation itself, but in the absence of a learning objective that separates structured signal from noise. Handcrafted reductions partially address this problem but impose rigid assumptions about what constitutes signal, discarding weak yet informative structure while retaining artifacts that fit the reduction model. Once removed, such information cannot be recovered by supervised learning. We propose an alternative: structure is the component of a radar spectrum that remains reconstructable under localized corruption, while noise is the component that does not. This perspective motivates self-supervised masked modeling for dense radar spectra without predefined signal-noise separation. We instantiate it with Leakage-aware Spectral MAE, a masked autoencoder for raw radar cubes using a corruption strategy inspired by cross-bin energy spreading caused by spectral leakage. We also demonstrate the importance of Doppler organization: treating Doppler as a primary spatial dimension in an x-y-D representation, with height encoded as features, substantially outperforms naive channel stacking and enables effective learning from dense 4D spectra. Without handcrafted reductions, our approach consistently outperforms state-of-the-art radar-based detectors and point-cloud-based self-supervised learning (SSL) counterparts. These results establish raw radar spectra as an effective representation for 3D perception when paired with an appropriate pretraining objective.</p></details> | <details><summary>19 pa...</summary><p>19 pages, 5 figures, 18 tables. Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Miao Zhang and Mohamed Abdelsamad contributed equally</p></details> |
| **[Sharp Regret Bounds and a Task-Covariance Correction for Spectral Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15825v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spectral contrastive learning keeps the $k$ directions along which two views of the data depend most strongly, while downstream tasks may need other directions. Existing guarantees for spectral features assume that the distribution of tasks has no preferred direction. We show that this isotropy condition is the wrong criterion. With linear probing, the task distribution affects the average probing gain only through the matrix $BΛB^\top$, where $B$ records the dependence between views and $Λ$ is the task covariance: optimal features span its top-$k$ eigenspace, whereas spectral features span that of $BB^\top$. Transfer therefore depends on alignment, and strongly uneven task preferences cost nothing when they favour the spectral directions. Otherwise the regret of spectral features is at most linear in the anisotropy of $Λ$. No representation chosen without task information improves this rate when the leading spectrum is flat, and a spectral gap makes it quadratic. With labelled tasks, the same matrix gives a correction: Fisher discriminant directions for a frozen encoder, or a one-term change to the spectral contrastive loss. We bound the regret of the correction by the error in estimating $BΛB^\top$, and compare this error with the misalignment of spectral features to decide when to correct. The analysis extends to finite samples, continuous variables and noisy views. Correction reduces synthetic held-out regret from $0.858$ to $0.003$ with $2000$ labelled tasks, and the decision rule gives the lowest regret among the tested rules at every bank size. On CIFAR-100, spectral features of frozen encoders keep most of the task signal on unseen classes, and where a bank of other classes would lower accuracy, the rule keeps them in every case.</p></details> | <details><summary>45 pa...</summary><p>45 pages, 4 figures, 15 tables. Feedback and comments are welcome!</p></details> |
| **[Towards Multiscale Molecular Representation Learning through Geometry-Based Fragmentation for Polymer Property Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07998v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>It is well established that molecular substructures can contribute unequally to chemical and physical properties, with specific motifs or side chains often playing dominant roles. To address these limitations, we formulate molecular fragmentation as a radius-constrained optimization problem inspired by the k-center formulation, where the number of clusters is not fixed a priori but emerges from the specified radius, and propose a geometry-based fragmentation method that partitions arbitrary molecular or material structures using largely geometric information, without relying on predefined chemical fragmentation rules. Building on this framework, we develop a geometric fragment-based attention mechanism that integrates multiscale structural information and incorporate it into a multimodal machine learning model combining p-SMILES representations with three-dimensional (3D) conformational information. | **[PAIR: Proprioception-Aligned Image-Ray Representations for Viewpoint-Robust Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11798v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>While imitation learning has made substantial progress toward generalizable robot manipulation through scaling, viewpoint robustness remains a major bottleneck. | **[The Dark Matter of Clinical Data: An Identification Limit on Disentangling Physiology from Care Process in electronic health records](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11726v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Representations learned from electronic health record (EHR) data encode patient physiology together with the clinical process that decides what is measured, when, and for whom. We formalize the data-generating process with latent physiology, ordering intent, laboratory values that exist only when measured, a learned representation, and clinical environments, and analyze two strategies for undoing this entanglement. First, no division of the information carried by laboratory "value" and "ordering" channels is identified from a single dataset: when ordering depends on the analyte, its distribution among untested patients is not recoverable, and a sentinel code for the undefined recorded value makes it carry all the entropy of the ordering indicator, fixing partial information decomposition (PID) for every redundancy measure satisfying the Williams-Beer axioms and invariant under injective re-encoding. Second, cross-site invariance of representation-conditional outcomes is a falsifiable implication of biological invariance taken jointly with exclusion and representation-sufficiency conditions, but not an identification strategy: we construct observationally equivalent models that disagree about biological invariance, whether the invariance test fails or passes. For binary physiology this happens exactly when case mix differs, the representation is lossy, and physiology affects the outcome. A worked example yields a 9.6-percentage-point cross-site gap under an unchanged biological mechanism. Tests performed for every patient remove the ordering gate, and measured tests can restore identification: biological invariance is identified when a test exactly summarizes outcome-relevant physiology, or, for binary physiology, when two tests with independent errors are available at sites with different case mix. Cross-site analyses should be reported as tests of a stated joint hypothesis.</p></details> | <details><summary>24 pa...</summary><p>24 pages, 3 figures, 3 tables. Supplementary Information and code are provided as ancillary files. Interactive companion with narrated explainers: https://criticaldata.github.io/Dark_Matter/</p></details> |
| **[Learning Pathology-Sensitive Representations for Unsupervised Retinal OCT Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33143v2)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Deep learning approaches for retinal optical coherence tomography (OCT) predominantly rely on predefined disease categories, limiting their ability to detect rare or previously unseen abnormalities. We systematically investigate the influence of pretraining strategies, anatomy-aware representation adaptation, and anomaly scoring mechanisms on the detection and localization of unseen retinal abnormalities. Our findings demonstrate that adapting pretrained representations to normal retinal structure improves anomaly sensitivity, while pixel-space reconstruction errors and feature-space deviations provide complementary signals for identifying pathological changes. These findings underscore the importance of anatomy-aware representation learning and offer insights into developing generalizable retinal anomaly detection frameworks beyond predefined disease categories.</p></details> |  |
| **[Self-Supervised Collapse Prevention with Score-Based Entropy Maximization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11379v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Diffusion models have demonstrated the effectiveness of score-based objectives for generative modeling, yet their potential for self-supervised representation learning remains underexplored. We introduce Entropy-Based Regularization (EBReg), a framework that uses latent score estimation to regularize the latent distribution during self-supervised training. EBReg jointly learns an encoder and a latent score model, and utilizes the learned score to provide a diversity-preserving signal that prevents representational collapse. Motivated by a mutual-information lower bound, we formulate the objective as view alignment with smoothed marginal entropy maximization and optimize the marginal entropy without explicit density evaluation. The same score model can additionally be used at test time to refine corrupted representations toward the training latent distribution. Empirically, EBReg learns competitive representations on ImageNet-1K, achieving 75.57% linear-probe accuracy with ViT-L. Across diverse architectures, score-guided test-time adaptation further improves robustness on ImageNet-C, yielding an average relative accuracy gain of approximately 5%.</p></details> |  |
| **[RCT-AD: Reliability-Aware Context and Temporal BEV Reasoning for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04689v2)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reliable temporal representation learning is important for end-to-end autonomous driving, particularly when visual observations are degraded by occlusion, motion blur, illumination variation, and sensor noise. Existing Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) approaches provide a unified representation for perception and planning; however, temporal features are commonly aggregated across frames without explicitly accounting for the reliability of individual observations. Consequently, low-quality or inconsistent features may propagate through temporal representations and degrade downstream perception, prediction, and planning. To address this problem, we propose RCT-AD, a Reliability-Aware Context and Temporal BEV Reasoning framework for end-to-end autonomous driving that explicitly models feature reliability and temporal consistency within a shared BEV representation. RCT-AD introduces a Reliable Context Awareness (RCA) module that estimates frame-level reliability using spatial, semantic, temporal, and visual-quality cues, and selectively preserves trustworthy information through a reliability-guided temporal memory mechanism. When the current observation is unreliable, historical high-reliability features are aligned and integrated to construct a more stable temporal BEV representation. A Temporal Trajectory Planner models long-term temporal dependencies and multi-agent interactions, while semantic segmentation and motion-guided detection provide complementary supervision for refining the shared BEV representation. Experiments on the nuScenes benchmark demonstrate consistent improvements across perception, motion prediction, and trajectory planning. RCT-AD achieves 61.5 NDS, 52.9 mAP, and 52.3 mIoU while maintaining competitive computational efficiency. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of reliability-aware temporal BEV reasoning for unified perception, prediction, and planning.</p></details> | <details><summary>15 pa...</summary><p>15 pages, 6 figures. Substantially revised version with an updated title, revised methodology, expanded technical descriptions, updated experimental analysis, and reorganized manuscript structure. The central research objective remains unchanged</p></details> |
| **[ClusLoad: Cluster-Guided Model Sharing for Scalable Distribution Transformer Load Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11170v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Large-scale device-level load forecasting for distribution transformers is challenged by substantial cross-device heterogeneity and the deployment burden of maintaining device-specific forecasting models. The proposed framework reduces mean absolute error (MAE) by approximately 20% relative to the direct PatchTST baseline, achieves a high-load warning accuracy of 95.66%, while requiring only seven cluster-specific forecasting models. These results demonstrate that behavior-based device organization provides an effective balance between forecasting accuracy, deployment scalability, and risk-oriented operational support for distribution transformer management.</p></details> |  |
| **[SurgWorld: Learning Surgical Representations Beyond Observation via Latent Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11140v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Surgical procedures are inherently partially observable, requiring surgeons to continuously infer evolving anatomical dynamics and future surgical states beyond the current observation. Inspired by latent predictive world models, we introduce SurgWorld, a video-native, self-supervised framework for surgical representation learning, which treats a specified spatial or temporal shift as a pseudo-action and learns to predict the target latent representation from a partial observation. Specifically, we propose Out-of-Sight Dynamics Modeling (OSDM) as the pretraining objective for predicting representations conditioned on relative spatio-temporal shifts. We further regularize the learned latent dynamics from OSDM with a Geometric Visual Similarity (GVS) objective by aligning patch-level relational structures between predicted and target representations, preserving spatial geometry and local visual coherence for dense perception. These two objectives are integrated through an agglomerative training framework that consolidates complementary supervisory signals from multiple teachers to learn a unified latent representation of surgical dynamics and visual geometry. Extensive transfer learning experiments on 17 surgical benchmarks show that SurgWorld consistently outperforms existing vision foundation models across surgical workflow recognition, fine-grained action understanding, and dense visual perception. We further conduct tailored qualitative and quantitative analyses, demonstrating that SurgWorld effectively captures surgical dynamics beyond the directly observed context through latent prediction.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication</p></details> |
| **[A Hierarchical Tokenization Framework for Voxel-Level fMRI Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11081v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce HBAR (Hierarchical Brain Activity Representation), a hierarchical tokenization framework for learning compact, multi-scale representations of neuroimaging data. On HCP-YA, at approximately 415x compression, HBAR achieves higher parcel and network correlations than rate-matched flat tokenizers, while at a lower 52x compression rate, shuffling the anatomical grouping reduces parcel correlation from 0.920 to 0.886. Code available at https://github.com/su-karanps/HBAR</p></details> |
| **[Elastic Representations via Hyperbolic Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11001v1)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Learned representations (or embeddings) are the foundation of modern machine learning systems, yet they are typically trained as fixed-size embeddings, without accounting for varying downstream resource or task constraints. Additionally, all prior works restrict their framework towards compression only. In this work, we introduce Hyperbolic Elastic Representation Learning (HERL), a framework for learning dimension-adaptive embeddings (compression and expansion) through a lightweight adaptor without retraining the base encoder. Our key idea is to exploit the exponential capacity of hyperbolic geometry by learning a non-linear mapping from the encoder space to hyperbolic space. We train a simple MLP to downsample the representations before projecting them onto a Lorentzian and learn adaptive representations that respect the Lorentzian geometry. Our approach is model-agnostic and effective in both supervised and unsupervised settings. Through extensive experiments across image and text modalities, we show that HERL preserves downstream performance across a wide range of embedding sizes, exhibits smooth interpolation between dimensions, and achieves these benefits at a fraction of the computational cost. Code: https://github.com/arjunrkaushik/herl.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at NeurIPS 2026</p></details> |
| **[Anatomy-Activated Mixture-of-Experts for 3D Medical Vision-Language Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10960v1)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Medical Vision-Language Pre-training (VLP) leverages the semantic richness of radiology reports to learn 3D representations, yet current architectures remain fundamentally anatomy-agnostic. While clinical diagnostics rely on organ-specific context, standard encoders apply a uniform, dense parameterization to all volumetric patches, and existing Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routers gate on isolated local features without explicit access to the organ composition of the scan or crop. We propose Anatomy-Activated Mixture-of-Experts (A$^{2}$MoE), a framework that incorporates anatomical inductive biases within the model's computational path. A$^{2}$MoE introduces two key components: (1) a Histogram Representation Router (HRR) that conditions expert selection on global visual context and a predicted anatomical composition histogram, encouraging anatomy-dependent expert weighting; and (2) an Organ-Query Multi-Scale Projector (OQMSP) that utilizes learnable queries to extract organ-level embeddings for fine-grained alignment without requiring manual annotations at inference. By routing at the patch level rather than the token level, A$^{2}$MoE reduces routing complexity by orders of magnitude while satisfying load balancing by construction. Pre-trained on over 60,000 multi-phase CT scans, A$^{2}$MoE achieves state-of-the-art performance across segmentation, classification, and detection benchmarks. Furthermore, we demonstrate that our anatomically grounded inductive biases support robust generalization to MRI and ultrasound, establishing a scalable foundation for medical representation learning. The code is available at https://github.com/gmum/A2MoE.</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[Rank-Transformed Dissimilarity Profiles for High-Dimensional Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.15199v4)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Despite advances in representation learning, high-dimensional classification remains challenging in low-sample-size regimes, where the dominant signal may vary across applications and labeled data are often limited. The key idea is to turn an effect of the curse of dimensionality into signal: high-dimensional geometry can induce systematic within-class and between-class dissimilarity patterns under location, scale, or other distributional changes, and these patterns are captured by the class-wise profiles. Building on this representation, we introduce a rank-transformed algorithm that converts dissimilarities into class-wise rank profiles. To clarify the underlying mechanism, we characterize the mean and covariance of a distance-based surrogate, showing how location, scale, and higher-order distributional features enter the profile representation. Contamination experiments show that the rank transformation can improve robustness. | **[Learning Biological Hierarchies in Single-Cell Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10838v1)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Cell types are organized by taxonomic relationships that define lineages in the so-called cell ontology. Using Lowest Common Ancestor (LCA) distances from the ontology graph, our framework pairs efficient offline triplet sampling with hierarchical cell-ontology learning. | **[Data Diversity, Not Frequency Invariance: A Controlled and Self-Audited Study of Compression-Robust Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28685v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Frequency features and compression-invariant representation learning are widely assumed to be key to deepfake detection that survives video compression. We test this with CAFRL - block-DCT and FFT-phase streams, compression-level-conditioned band attention, and adversarial (gradient-reversal) compression invariance - and report a controlled negative. Under a pre-registered protocol with capacity- and augmentation-matched controls, a plain EfficientNet-B0 on multi-quality data beat CAFRL as specified at every compression level on the FaceForensics++ test split, by 3.66 AUC points at CRF 40 (paired, single seed). A self-audit of our own negative found four defects biased against the frequency hypothesis, and pre-specified re-tests repairing all four showed the deficit to be a recipe artifact, not an architecture failure: the baseline recipe recovered 3.96 points over the matching shipped-recipe variant. The frequency path made no detectable difference: discriminative alone (standalone validation AUC 0.91-0.98 late in training) but of no marginal value under this fusion, at two feature widths of one 4.0 M trunk, every seed-pooled interval for the intra-dataset compression contrasts including zero; on the single held-out manipulation tested, the fair variants sat below the plain backbone. The adversarial branch, as specified, added nothing and degraded its own conditioning estimator; at the fair recipe it is untested. Robustness under single-pass H.264 re-encoding came instead from data diversity: real constant-rate-factor variants beat synthetic JPEG augmentation by 7.3 points (single runs, non-overlapping intervals). The evidence is FaceForensics++-family, GAN-era and single-codec. Match controls on training recipe as well as capacity, and buy compression robustness with codec diversity before architecture.</p></details> | <details><summary>38 pa...</summary><p>38 pages, 4 figures. Submitted to IEEE Access. Code, per-video predictions and a claims-to-artifacts map: https://github.com/Capta1n-n9m0/cafrl-negative (v1.0.0); archival deposit: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22032529</p></details> |
| **[How reliable is an embedding of the seabed? Repeat-look measurement of frozen encoders on synthetic aperture sonar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10770v1)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Pretrained encoders from self-supervised representation learning are increasingly applied to sonar imagery of the seafloor, where they are evaluated with protocols that take one embedding per target. A target patch of seabed, by contrast, is re-imaged from an alternate look on overlapping survey passes, so each embedding is one measurement of that target. Standard protocols cannot say how consistent those measurements are, or how much of their spread belongs to the patch and how much to the acquisition geometry. We treat this as a measurement-reliability problem. Using a synthetic-aperture sonar (SAS) survey, we measure the repeat-look reliability of embeddings from a frozen natural-image encoder (DINOv3 ViT-S/16). Standard single-look metrics, effective rank, clustering and a linear probe for coral, are all stable to the look, and the probe performs well (AUC 0.93). Yet the repeat-look reliability of the same embeddings is poor, with a distance-based intraclass correlation (dbICC) of 0.20 and nearest neighbours and clusters that barely agree across looks. This contradiction resolves as we find the probe learns a stable direction, whose ICC of 0.60 is 3.0 times that of the full embedding. Variance components, calculated from generalizability theory, attribute under a tenth of the within-cell spread to look acquisition geometry. The rest is cell-specific and cannot be separated without repeat passes at matched geometry. A sonar-pretrained encoder (BenthicDINO) and a classical SAS texture descriptor show the same pattern. For this dataset and the tested representations, uses that depend on patch identity fail, such as cross-pass comparison and retrieval, while uses at the level of habitat type (e.g. coral classification) do not. Because the standard metrics did not detect this, we argue that repeat-look reliability should accompany any evaluation of representations of the seafloor.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at the Maritime Computer Vision Workshop (MaCVi) at WACV 2027</p></details> |
| **[Laplacian Heads Improve Transformers by Smoothing Token Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09297v4)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transformers update token representations through multi-head attention and residual connections: $X \leftarrow X + \sum_{i} P^{(i)}\text{Norm}(X)W_{V_i}W_{o_i}$, where $P^{(i)}$ is the softmax probability matrix in head $i$. We propose replacing some $P^{(i)}$'s with $I - P^{(i)}$, giving $X \leftarrow X + \sum_{i \in \mathcal{A}} P^{(i)}\text{Norm}(X)W_{V_i}W_{o_i} + \sum_{i \in \mathcal{L}} (I - P^{(i)})\text{Norm}(X)W_{V_i}W_{o_i}$. We call heads that use $I - P^{(i)}$ Laplacian heads. Our proposal has two motivations. First, it allows attention heads to update the mean of token representations, while Laplacian heads can directly control within-sequence variance. Second, if tokens are viewed as nodes in a graph with normalized edge weights $P^{(i)}$, then $I - P^{(i)}$ is a graph Laplacian operator, and the update can be interpreted as one step of heat diffusion on the graph. We show that this simple modification improves performance across supervised learning, causal language modeling, and vision self-supervised learning tasks. To investigate why, we examine the token representations learned with and without Laplacian heads. In supervised learning, Laplacian heads collapse token representations within the same sequence and align the sequence means with the geometry of Neural Collapse. In language modeling, they increase the separability of token representations that share the same next-token prediction. In vision self-supervised learning, they produce token representations whose top principal components better capture segmentation structure. Across modalities, they also lead to faster-decaying spectra, indicating stronger token smoothing. Overall, our findings challenge the prevailing view that token oversmoothing is inherently harmful, showing instead that certain forms of smoothing can be beneficial.</p></details> |  |
| **[DTA-GT: Direction- and Topology-Aware Graph Transformer for Neural Network Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16822v3)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Architecture attribute predictors reduce the cost of neural architecture search by estimating accuracy or latency from a small set of evaluated candidates. Neural architectures are operation-labeled directed acyclic graphs (DAGs), where edge directions determine computational roles and execution dependencies. Existing graph Transformer predictors incorporate topological and directional information in different ways, but primarily focus on specific mechanisms rather than coordinated encoder-wide modeling of these semantics. We introduce encoder-wide directional consistency, a design principle that coordinates the modeling of directed computational semantics across node initialization, global spectral encoding, pairwise interaction, and feature updates. To implement this principle, we propose DTA-GT, a Direction- and Topology-Aware Graph Transformer that integrates direction- and topology-aware node initialization, magnetic Laplacian spectral encoding, direction-dual structural attention, and a Direction-Sensitive MoE-FFN. Across accuracy and latency prediction on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201, DTA-GT consistently outperforms representative RNN-, CNN-, GNN-, Transformer-, and hybrid predictors under limited-budget settings. Ablations and magnetic spectral controls support the contribution of the direction-aware stages and orientation-sensitive spectral information, while mechanistic diagnostics characterize module behavior. These results support encoder-wide directional consistency as an effective design principle for architecture DAG representation.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 38 pages, 6 figures</p></details> |
| **[Chem-PerturBridge: a harmonized compendium of small molecule perturbation transcriptomic effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31522v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Large perturbation models require training data encompassing chemical, cellular, and assay diversity. We use the resource to evaluate matched-condition agreement across datasets and replicate agreement within datasets. However, they generally exceed individual same-context different-compound signatures, while often falling below the smoothed centroid of such signatures. We further evaluate Chem-PerturBridge as a pretraining resource for compound representation learning. Under a compound-held-out OP3 evaluation split, embeddings pretrained on Chem-PerturBridge improve over L1000-only embeddings, Morgan fingerprints, chemical language model embeddings, and the descriptor-free OP3 baseline across metrics. An extensive molecule-holdout evaluation across 11 datasets further shows that models trained on Chem-PerturBridge outperform or match those that are not. | **[Decompose Dynamics Before Learning Dependencies in Spatiotemporal Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36637v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Relations in networked spatiotemporal systems are often learned from observations that entangle dynamics governed by different mechanisms, obscuring what evolves locally and how it propagates across nodes. We introduce Component-Aware Network Dynamics with Ordered Relations (CANDOR), which decomposes local dynamics before learning their dependencies. CANDOR represents each trajectory through a persistent background, gradual accumulation and release, and sparse shocks. Conditioned on these components, a delay-aware physical branch models edge and sample-dependent propagation over directed topology, while a topology-unconstrained functional branch discovers latent dependencies from background dynamics. Context-adaptive fusion combines functional, forward-propagation, and reverse-support forecasts, with training objectives encouraging specialized and semantically consistent representations. Experiments on two traffic benchmarks and three long-horizon water-quality datasets span two distinct spatiotemporal systems: human-driven urban traffic and naturally evolving river water quality. CANDOR achieves the lowest aggregate normalized error across five datasets, outperforming the overall runner-up by 7.25%. These results establish decomposition before dependency learning as an effective principle for spatiotemporal representation learning.</p></details> |  |
| **[LEMON-ZEST: Evolution-Informed Tokenization for Efficient Protein Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37675v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Protein Language Models (PLMs) have made remarkable progress following scaling laws established in natural language processing across sequence- and structure-based tasks, yet the potential of tokenization remains underexploited. Our results demonstrate that evolution-informed tokenization can substitute for massive parameter scaling, opening a new direction for efficient, biologically-grounded protein representation learning. | **[Entropy Guided Dynamic Patch Segmentation for Time Series Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26157v3)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Patch-based transformers have emerged as efficient and improved long-horizon modeling architectures for time series modeling. Yet, existing approaches rely on temporally-agnostic patch construction, where arbitrary starting positions and fixed lengths fracture temporal coherence by splitting natural transitions across boundaries. | **[Learning Reusable Behavioral Motifs for Continuous Animal Behavior Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15190v4)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Natural behavior evolves continuously and often reuses movement structure across contexts, but existing behavioral segmentation methods typically assign a single discrete syllable at each time point or impose restrictive generative assumptions. This formulation avoids hard behavioral boundaries while capturing continuity and compositionality. Across simulated gridworld and continuous-control tasks, mouse maze navigation and pose data, MCD identifies reusable motifs that explain task and temporal variation. MCD therefore provides a flexible and interpretable framework for computational behavior analysis. Code is available in https://github.com/G1NO3/MCD.</p></details> | <details><summary>10 pa...</summary><p>10 pages and 5 figures for the main text</p></details> |
| **[Spectral Ghost in Representation Learning: A Unified View from Component Analysis to Self-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20154v3)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Self-supervised learning (SSL) extracts representations from massive unlabeled data that transfer to a variety of downstream tasks. Its empirical success has produced a dizzying array of objectives and procedures, yet a clear understanding of what representation is desired, and why these seemingly different methods work, is still missing. In this paper, we develop a unified spectral framework for representation learning. We first show that a representation is sufficient for arbitrary downstream prediction with a linear head if it spans the spectral decomposition of the pointwise mutual information, which can be extracted purely from unlabeled data. | **[Semantic Prior-Guided Incomplete Cross-Modal Retrieval with Noisy Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06113v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Cross-modal retrieval (CMR) aims to bridge the semantic gaps between modalities by learning shared representations. Specifically, SPIRN uses fixed semantic priors as a consistent reference for robust representation learning, noisy-label handling, and missing-modality completion. By integrating attention mechanisms and nonlinear features with an explicit probabilistic spatial random field, the hybrid model captured the relational dependence, consistently improved predictive accuracy, and provided more realistic uncertainty quantification in both simulation and applied settings. | **[Dataset Recommendation for Heterogeneous and Dynamic Scholarly Graphs via Multimodal Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05071v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Researchers struggle to find relevant datasets amid the growing volume of data across repositories and scholarly publications. However, this task remains challenging because Scholarly Knowledge Graphs (SKGs) are large-scale, heterogeneous, dynamic, and incomplete.
